# TON Family (Plan 6 of 7) Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Add the built-in TON family (chain `ton`, networks mainnet and testnet) on `@ton/ton` + `@ton/core`: v4r2 and v5r1 wallets with their full identity, native and jetton transfers with memos and batches, `seqno` ordering with `valid_until` expiry, TEP-467 message-hash Attempts resolved to canonical transactions, finality on masterchain inclusion plus a completed message trace, proofs, address history, the toncenter presets, a verified token catalog and the `crypto-aio/ton` entry.

**Architecture:** One driver core (`src/adapters/ton/{api,network,fees,errors,trace,decode,reader,builder,proofs,driver}.ts`) talks REST to two core transports under each call's tags: toncenter API v2 (`rpc`: live liteserver state, get-methods at a fixed masterchain block, fee emulation, sending) and API v3 (`indexer`: message → transaction, traces, history, jetton metadata). `@ton/core` and `@ton/ton` do codec work only (`wallets.ts`, `messages.ts`: wallet contracts, `StateInit`, signing messages, bodies, BOCs) and supply the `crypto-aio/native` `TonClient`, bridged through its `httpAdapter` to the transport. The family shape is Plan 2's: `tonDriverFactory(makeNative)` in `driver.ts`, and the library module `native-client.ts` exports the loadable `tonLibraryDriverFactory`. Everything is tested offline against a scripted toncenter node that models wallets, seqno, expiry, bounces, jettons, traces and indexer lag.

**Tech Stack:** TypeScript 5.9.3 (CommonJS, `module`/`moduleResolution: node16`); Node ≥ 22; Jest 30 + ts-jest 29.4.12; pnpm 10.5.2; `@ton/ton` `16.3.0`, `@ton/core` `0.63.1`, `@ton/crypto` `3.3.0` (exact devDependencies; optional peerDependencies `^16.3.0`, `^0.63.1`, `^3.3.0`); `@noble/curves` 1.9 (already a dependency).

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-23-blockchain-adapter-layer-design.md`

**Also read:** `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-23-plan-1-core-handoff.md` (§3 adapter obligations bind this plan), the `ChainDriver` contract table in `src/core/driver/types.ts`, and Plan 2 (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-25-plan-2-evm.md`), whose final shape of the composition root, `package.json`, the guides and `src/testing/generation.ts` this plan builds on.

**Plan series:** Plan 1 (core, merged), Plan 2 (EVM), Plan 2.5 (core prerequisites for Plans 3–6, ruling A11; it lifts this plan's Task 1), Plans 3–5 (UTXO, Tron, Solana), **Plan 6 (this document: TON)**, Plan 7 (release). Plans 2 and 2.5 merge before this plan runs.

## Global Constraints

- Runtime: "Node.js ≥ 22, backend only" (`engines.node >= 22`). Toolchain pinned: "TypeScript 5.9", "Jest 30 + ts-jest", "ESLint 9 flat config".
- "The core (`src/core/**`) imports no blockchain SDK; SDKs are optional peer dependencies loaded lazily." `src/core/**` must not import `src/adapters/**` or `src/testing/**`.
- "Each family ships an SDK-free **plugin** module (`src/adapters/<family>/plugin.ts`)"; "A manifest is static metadata plus `load()`. Only `load()` pulls in the adapter module and, with it, the SDK." "Lazy loading uses `require()` inside `load()`, not `import()`." Only `wallets.ts`, `messages.ts`, `reader.ts` … `driver.ts` and `native-client.ts` (everything the library module reaches) may import `@ton/*`, and every `require()` names a bare peer package (R80); `types.ts`, `chains.ts`, `presets.ts`, `tokens.ts`, `address.ts`, `api.ts`, `network.ts`, `fees.ts`, `errors.ts` and `plugin.ts` are SDK-free.
- "`@tonconnect/sdk` | **Replaced** by `@ton/ton` + `@ton/core`." `Library.TON` is `'@ton/ton'`, the only TON library id.
- "Hard `dependencies`: `@noble/curves`, `@noble/hashes`, `@scure/base`, `@scure/bip32`, `debug`" (plus `@scure/bip39`). No new runtime dependency.
- "Optional `peerDependencies` … `@ton/ton`, `@ton/core`. … All of them are also `devDependencies`, pinned to those tested versions." Here `@ton/ton` `16.3.0` / `^16.3.0`, `@ton/core` `0.63.1` / `^0.63.1`, plus `@ton/crypto` `3.3.0` / `^3.3.0`, which both SDKs declare as a peer (D2).
- "If a lazy load fails because the SDK is missing ⇒ `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` with the exact install command."
- Plan 2's final family shape binds (R79–R82): `TON_PEER_DEPENDENCIES` keyed by package name and equal to `package.json`'s peer ranges; a `lazy.test.ts` that loads each manifest in its own module registry and asserts the loaded SDKs equal its peer names; the augmentation test in both file orders with `USE_ACME`; the SDK-free main-entry guard extended to `@ton/*`.
- "`exports`: `.`, …, `./ton`, `./testing`, `./native` (CJS + `.d.ts`; `typesVersions` for older resolvers)."
- "SDKs never see real URLs or API keys; secrets stay in the transport." The toncenter key travels in an `X-API-Key` header whose value is a `Secret`.
- "Drivers never hold keys and never sign." "Drivers are shared across tenants, so they hold no wallet, tenant or operation state", except caches of immutable chain data (jetton wallet addresses, jetton metadata).
- Store records hold plain data only (R11): `UnsignedTx`, `SignedTx`, orderings, `fee.details` and observations never carry SDK objects (cells travel as base64 BOC strings).
- "Events carry only `operational`-class data." Driver reasons are short fixed texts with no addresses, amounts or node text (R24).
- "`bigint` always means base units": nanograms for the native coin, the jetton's base units for jettons.
- "TON | account / `seqno` + expiry"; "Batch outputs | wallet-version limit (v4r2: 4, v5r1: 255)"; "`ton` forward+gas; jetton `attached` value with refunded excess → `upper`"; "1 × `ed25519` over signing cell hash"; "`message-hash` after signing (**not canonical**; tx hash resolved later)"; "masterchain inclusion **and** completed message trace"; "Replace / cancel: no / no"; "Block scan: **no** (sharded)"; "Address history: yes (toncenter)"; "Tokens: Jetton"; "Memo: yes (text comment)" (spec §15).
- "For TON, the intent's `to` variant decides bounce behaviour. The driver never silently changes it." (spec §6.4)
- Every I/O call carries the tags of the `ChainDriver` contract table (purpose, retry class, quorum, fanout, signal) (R41). No driver request path waits on a real timer; the Tasks 5, 11 and 13 suites must pass 100 consecutive runs (R46).
- "Unit tests make no network calls; a guard fails any test that tries." "Integration (opt-in): `CRYPTO_AIO_INTEGRATION=1` runs read-only testnet checks." Environment variables carry flags and routing only, never keys.
- Process: work on the TON execution branch cut from `main` after Plans 2 and 2.5 merged (skip Task 1 once Plan 2.5 carries it); TDD for pure logic (red, green, commit); stage explicit paths only; never stage `.claude/`, `.superpowers/` or `.env`; never push. **Before every commit run `pnpm format && pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test`** (add `pnpm doc` when docs or exports change). Prettier may reflow code copied from this plan; that is expected.
- Every commit message ends with the trailer `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>`.

## Review Focus

These five inputs are the most likely to bite a real user, and no spec example exercises them. Each has a pinned test in the task named.

1. **A transfer the wallet skipped.** Send mode +2 (IGNORE_ERRORS) is mandatory, so when the balance no longer covers a message at execution time, the wallet transaction *succeeds*, consumes the seqno and sends nothing. It must end `failed` (`TX_REVERTED`, reason `the wallet skipped a message`), never `final` (lesson 7). Pinned in Task 7 ("fails a request whose message the wallet skipped") and Task 13 ("fails a transfer the wallet could not pay for").
2. **Indexer lag after the seqno is consumed.** The liteserver already shows the seqno consumed at finality while toncenter v3 has not indexed the transaction: the Attempt must stay undecided, never `replaced`, not even an observed one (lesson 16, I4). Pinned in Task 10 ("decides nothing while the indexer lags behind the consumed seqno") and Task 13 ("waits out indexer lag without a false verdict", which reads the status on every pass).
3. **A bounceable transfer to an uninitialized recipient.** A raw or `EQ…` address to a fresh wallet bounces the value back: `failed` with `transfer bounced`; the `UQ…` form of the same recipient lands and is `final`. Pinned in Task 7 ("fails a bounced native transfer") and Task 13 ("honours the recipient's bounce flag").
4. **A v5r1 wallet on testnet.** The SDK's default wallet id is mainnet's; a v5r1 wallet must derive its testnet address from `-3`, and a configured `networkGlobalId` of another network must fail with `CONFIG_INVALID` before any key is used (lesson 5). Pinned in Task 3 ("refuses a v5r1 wallet id of another network") and Task 11 ("derives v5r1 wallets from the network's global id").
5. **Two transfers racing on one wallet.** The second must wait (`SEQUENCE_BUSY`) until the first is included or proven dead; an Attempt the chain never saw becomes `expired` only once every shard's time is past its `valid_until`. Pinned in Task 10 ("proves expiry only once every shard is past valid_until") and Task 13 ("keeps one wallet strictly serial").

## Decisions recorded by the plan author

The common brief, the Plan 6 scope rulings and controller rulings A8/A9 (Proposals P6-1, P6-2) and lessons 1–17 bind this plan. Where they and the spec were silent, these decisions were made; each names what it costs if wrong.

- **D1. REST straight to the transport; `TonClient` is only the native client.** Every driver call is one `transport.http` request under that call's tags. toncenter's `/api/v2/jsonRPC` answers carry no JSON-RPC `id`, so the core's `transport.rpc` refuses them, and `TonClient` runs axios with a request timeout (a real timer, R46). The SDKs do codec work only; `TonClient` (with an `httpAdapter` that posts to the transport, spec §11) is the `crypto-aio/native` client. Cost: the SDK's own request plumbing is never exercised by the driver.
- **D2. `@ton/crypto` is a third optional peer.** `@ton/ton` 16.3.0 and `@ton/core` 0.63.1 both declare `@ton/crypto >=3.2.0` as a peer, and `@ton/core` requires it at load time (cell hashing, `domainSign`). Spec §16 lists only `@ton/ton` and `@ton/core`; the install hint names all three. Cost: one more package in the `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` hint.
- **D3. Two transports, and the indexer is required.** `rpc` = toncenter API v2, `indexer` = API v3; the manifest sets `requiresIndexer: true` and adds `address-history` through `indexerCapabilities`. Spec §21 allows an indexer-less TON, where Attempts "stay at `submitted`/`included` without a trace-based `final`"; a payment driver that can never finalize is not useful, so the indexer is required (the `public` preset fills it, with the not-for-production warning). Cost: a user with only a liteserver-backed v2 endpoint must also configure a v3 endpoint.
- **D4. The native coin is Gram (ruling A16).** The coin formerly called Toncoin (TON) is Gram, ticker GRAM: ton.org shows "Gram (GRAM)", docs.ton.org says "Gram (GRAM) is the TON's primary cryptocurrency" and counts in nanograms, and the TON Foundation announced "Native Token Rename: Toncoin (TON) to Gram (GRAM)" (Appendix A). `nativeAsset: { symbol: 'GRAM', decimals: 9, name: 'Gram' }`, and the plugin registers the alias `TON` for the same asset id. The chain id stays `ton` and the library id `@ton/ton` (spec §2). Cost: spec §2's table says TON; both aliases resolve.
- **D5. The Attempt and the payload.** The payload is the unsigned external message (a BOC) whose wallet body holds 512 zero bits where the signature goes; the one `ed25519` request signs the signing message's 32-byte cell hash (`payloadKind: 'message'`: ed25519 signs these exact bytes). `assemble` puts the signature at the one position (front for v4r2, tail for v5r1) whose remainder hashes to the signed digest, so it needs no wallet version and cannot write anywhere else. The Attempt id is the TEP-467 normalized hash, lower-case hex, `idKind: 'message-hash'`, `canonical: false`; there is no `expectedRef`. Cost: none known; the layout is proven against live mainnet signatures (Appendix A).
- **D6. Send mode 3 on every message.** `PAY_GAS_SEPARATELY + IGNORE_ERRORS`. Without +2, W5 commits the request's seqno and then fails with exit code 137, so nothing moves (the scripted node models it, M11); TEP-467's uniqueness holds only with +2; and with it an accepted request always consumes its seqno. The price is Review Focus 1: a message the balance cannot cover is skipped silently, which only the trace verdict (D11) catches.
- **D7. Bounce follows the recipient's address (P6-1).** A friendly address's flag decides; a raw address is bounceable, as in `@ton/core`. Messages to the sender's own jetton wallet are always bounceable. The address variant (`bounceable`, `testOnly`, `urlSafe`) is part of the intent hash, so the same recipient written in two forms is two intents (a false idempotency conflict, in the safe direction). That includes the display-only flags `urlSafe` and, on testnet, `testOnly` (M10): the guide says so, and Plan 7 may hash only `bounceable`. Mainnet refuses testnet-only addresses (TEP-2); testnet accepts both flags. Two documented departures: a jetton transfer ignores the recipient's variant, because TEP-74 decides bounce on the jetton hop (M9); and unlike TEP-2's wallet rule (Tonkeeper), a `UQ…` address to an *active* contract is sent non-bounceable, as written (M14; a Plan 7 opt-in candidate).
- **D8. Chain time, not the local clock, for `valid_until`.** `build` reads the wallet state (`getAddressInformation`), and `valid_until = sync_utime + validForSeconds` (network param, default 60). A `sync_utime` more than 300 s from the local clock is a retryable `PROVIDER_INCONSISTENT`, and `valid_until` must fit 32 bits, so one bad read cannot set a lifetime the core must wait out (M3). The SDK's wall-clock fallback for a zero timeout is refused outright. Cost: a 60-s window is short for slow signers; `awaiting-signature` flows should raise `validForSeconds` (a network param) or expect `expired` and `rebuild`.
- **D9. Expiry is proven by shard time.** A wallet rejects a message once `valid_until <= now()`, where `now()` is the time of the block processing it. `expired(ordering)` takes the attested head H (D10), then quorum-reads every shard top block H commits (fixed ids), and is `true` only when `valid_until` is at or below the smallest `gen_utime` of H and those shard tops: every later block of every shard is at least that old. `expired` gets no address, so it cannot pick the wallet's own shard; the minimum over all shards is conservative. Cost: one `getShards` and one header read per shard, only once masterchain time has passed `valid_until`.
- **D10. Finality (lesson 17, final form).** A masterchain block is BFT-final once it exists, so `getFinalizedHeight` is the masterchain head (`monitor`). Proofs attest each fact at its own height with a monotone predicate or at a fixed block: "my seqno is above n" (`slotConsumed`), "I hold masterchain block R" (`reachedMasterchain`), state and headers at a fixed seqno. The one unanchored head (`finalizedHead`, and the block `expired`/`includedFinal` read state at) is one endpoint's head trailed by `finalitySkewBlocks` (network param, default 10 masterchain blocks, ~4 s, validated 1–1000) and attested by a quorum read of that block, keyed on its root and file hashes. When a healthy peer trails further, the read is retried once at the transport's lag tolerance (150, the furthest a proof endpoint may trail), so the attestation is always reachable (M1). By design `getFinalizedHeight` (the head) and `finalizedHead()` (the attested head minus the skew) differ. `observe` reports `success` only once the transaction's trace is complete; `includedFinal` decides only for a complete trace whose last block the rpc endpoints attest they hold, and otherwise throws a retryable `ProviderError('PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE')`, which decides nothing (lesson 16).
- **D11. The verdict: value moved, or it failed (lesson 7, lesson 15, the phantom-success rule's final wording).** On the verdict paths only (`observe`, `includedFinal`), an Attempt succeeded when (a) the wallet transaction's compute and action phases succeeded, (b) every message the signed request asked for is among its outgoing messages (a missing one is a skip, `failed`, only when the action phase itself counts that many skipped actions), (c) no native message came back bounced, and (d) every jetton request's hop sent an `internal_transfer` **from the sender of a positive amount** (never the exact amount: fee-on-transfer tokens exist), which the receiving jetton wallet executed, and the two jetton wallets are the master's own for the sender and the **intended recipient** (`get_wallet_address` under `PROOF`, I5; otherwise `failed`, `the jetton wallets are not the master’s`). Decoding reports the amount that actually moved. Anything else the chain itself records as a failure is `failed` with a fixed reason (P6-2). Lesson 18 (widened): an answer that contradicts the signed request, such as a message missing that the wallet did not skip, or our jetton wallet running the transfer yet sending nothing from the sender, throws a retryable `PROVIDER_INCONSISTENT` and decides nothing; a zero-amount `internal_transfer` still means nothing moved, so `failed` stays accurate there. Every input is bound (C1): the Attempt is the external message to `from` whose TEP-467 hash, computed locally from its body, is the Attempt id (the indexer's `hash_norm` only finds candidates); every message body read must hash to the `bodyHash` the quorum keyed, else a retryable `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`; the trace key covers every field of every transaction. `getTransaction` and `history` report what the chain did, for anyone's transactions.
- **D12. "Not included" only from attested state (lesson 16).** `includedFinal` first looks up the indexer by the Attempt's hash. When that finds nothing, it reads the wallet's seqno at the attested head H (a fixed block): unconsumed there → `included: false` only if the message is already expired at H (D9), else undecided; consumed → it searches the consuming request (external, or a proven relayed W5 one) in the wallet's transactions below its last transaction at H (a fixed lt window, `CONSUMER_PAGES` = 8 pages of 64), and computes that message's TEP-467 hash itself: ours → proven included, another → `included: false`, not indexed yet → undecided. The indexer's own `hash_norm` is never trusted, for a positive (C1) or a negative. The seqno at H comes from an `active` wallet, or an `uninitialized` one whose slot is seqno 0; any other state (frozen, deleted) is undecided, never "seqno 0" (I6). A W5 `internal_signed` request (a relayed, gasless transfer, M17) counts as a consumer only when its transaction's compute phase succeeded (the wallet ran it) and its signature verifies locally under the wallet's public key read at H, with a wallet id that derives `from` as a v5r1 wallet with that key (ruling A23): anyone can post such a body for about 0.01 GRAM, a v5r1 wallet ignores a forged one, and a v4r2 twin of the same key accepts any internal body. External requests are checked the same way, signature and wallet id (the final review): otherwise a lone lying indexer on a single-endpoint preset could make up an external consumer. `slotConsumed(…, 'latest')` answers `false`: masterchain state is final, so TON never reports an observed `replaced` for a landed message the indexer has not reached (I4). The broadcaster compares the node's `hash_norm` with the Attempt id and logs a mismatch by code only. Cost: a wallet that sent more than ~512 transactions between the Attempt and H keeps the Attempt undecided, logged as `SEQNO_CONSUMER_NOT_FOUND`. Core residual R76 (two separate proof calls in `whenAbsent`) is closed for TON given bound data: "unconsumed and expired at H" excludes a later inclusion of this message, and a consumer hashed locally is a direct proof.
- **D13. Fees.** The `network` charge is the v2 `estimateFee` emulation of the wallet request (import, gas, storage; signature check skipped) plus the forward fee of the outgoing messages, counted once (I3): the emulation's own `fwd_fee` when it is positive (it follows the real action list), else the full forward fee of every outgoing message from config params 24/25 (the formula of `@ton/ton`'s `computeFwdFees`); `details.forwardFeeSource` records which (`emulated` or `computed`). A jetton transfer adds an `attached` charge (`jettonAttached` × outputs, default 0.05 GRAM each, refunded above cost) and `bound: 'upper'`; a native transfer is `bound: 'expected'` (storage fees accrue). Speeds are accepted and change nothing: TON fees are fixed by config. `TonFeeOverride` is `{ attached }` for jettons only. Cost: a native estimate can be a little low; the wallet then skips the message and the verdict fails the Attempt honestly.
- **D14. Jettons.** The sender's jetton wallet is the master's `get_wallet_address(owner)`, resolved for sending under `PROOF` (the answer is immutable, so honest endpoints agree); the caches of jetton wallets and verified owners hold only quorum-attested answers, and a single endpoint's answer is never cached (I5). `forward_ton_amount` is `jettonForwardAmount` (default 1 nanogram, as docs.ton.org's payment setup), so the recipient always gets a notification and a memo rides in its `forward_payload`. Metadata: `get_jetton_data` must run (a missing account or TVM exit code is the token's own `ASSET_RESOLUTION`, lesson 13); decimals and symbol come from the on-chain content dictionary (TEP-64, decimals default 9), otherwise from the indexer's `jetton/masters` content and `metadata`. An inbound jetton movement is decoded only for a jetton wallet the master itself names for its owner (a fake jetton wallet can claim any master).
- **D15. Heights, hashes, locators.** Height = masterchain seqno; block hash = its root hash in hex; transaction and message hashes are lower-case hex (toncenter answers base64). `maxLagBlocks: 150` (~60 s of ~0.4-s masterchain blocks, measured). `reorgWindow: 16` is unused (no block source). Transfer locators: `msg:in` for an inbound internal message's value, `msg:in:jetton` for the jettons it brings (an `internal_transfer` on a verified jetton wallet, or a `transfer_notification` from one), `msg:<i>` for outbound message `i`. A non-bounceable deposit to a fresh address is credited though the chain marks it `aborted`: decoding reports it executed.
- **D16. Broadcast answers.** toncenter answers every refused message with HTTP 500, which the transport turns into an ambiguous failure; the broadcaster rethrows it unclassified (lesson 3), and such an Attempt ends `expired` (proven) after `valid_until`, so the user must `rebuild` (the guide says so). `classifyBroadcastError` covers only definitive 4xx answers of v2-compatible endpoints (the text is the JSON body's `error` field): the state-dependent patterns (seqno, expiry, wallet id, signature mode, funds, not deployed) are `refused` with their codes; after them, only the exact texts "duplicate external message" / "external message already known|in the queue" are `already-known` (anchored, M4); an anchored "failed to unpack/deserialize" is `rejected` (malformed bytes); everything else is `refused` (`TX_REFUSED`). Any definitive RPC error on a `proof` or `monitor` read, whatever its text, is a retryable `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` (M2, lesson 18 widened): TON's negatives come only from attested state (D12), never from an error; only `PROVIDER_MISCONFIGURED` surfaces as the configuration error it is.
- **D17. Wallet identity is explicit.** `wallets.<name>.ton = { version }` is required whenever a key is used; v4r2 `subwalletId` defaults to `698983191 + workchain`, v5r1 `subwalletNumber` to 0 and `networkGlobalId` to the network's (and must equal it). The build refuses (`CONFIG_INVALID`) a wallet whose derived address is not `from`. `limits()` is 4 (v4r2), 255 (v5r1), 1 only when the wallet has no `ton` identity; a malformed identity throws its `CONFIG_INVALID` (M18). A wallet key must be a canonical 32-byte ed25519 point, not of small order (M12). TON-native 24-word mnemonics are not BIP39: import the 32-byte ed25519 seed (`localSigner({ ed25519 })`); the guide says so.
- **D18. Memo.** A text comment (op 0, snake string), at most 1024 UTF-8 bytes (`INVALID_INTENT` above), on every output of the intent.
- **D19. The scripted toncenter node is test-only** (`test/adapters/ton/support/node.ts`), as Plan 2's D5: it parses messages with `@ton/core`, so shipping it would make `crypto-aio/testing` depend on an optional peer.
- **D20. Crash tests** use `fenceGeneration` and `type Generation` from `src/testing/generation.ts` (Plan 2 Task 11, landed) with `restart({ killPrevious: true })` (controller ruling on P3-A), per Plan 2's usage notes: a fresh `{ alive: true }` per generation; the kill sets `alive = false` and assembles a new container over the same raw clock, stores and signer; the killed container is never closed; a plain `restart()` leaves the old one running; and a test pins the kill (a call on the old handle never settles). No fence copy.
- **D21. No `block-scan`.** TON is sharded (spec §15): the driver has no `blocks`, and the capability is never advertised. Deposits are found with `history` or `waitForConfirmation`.
- **D22. Integration routing.** `CRYPTO_AIO_IT_TON_NETWORK` (`mainnet` or `testnet`, default `testnet`), and `CRYPTO_AIO_IT_TON_RPC_URL` / `CRYPTO_AIO_IT_TON_INDEXER_URL` (default: the keyless `public` preset; a URL carrying toncenter's `api_key` query parameter is a `Secret`). The test is read-only. Ruling A17 (Plan 2.5) makes health probes take tokens from an endpoint's rate limit, so the pauses between reads are only a courtesy to the keyless preset (one request per second). A12 (`exactIntegers`) is not needed: toncenter sends amounts as strings. R73 does not apply: TON has no cancel.

## File Structure

```
src/
  index.ts                         MODIFY: export DriverOutput (Task 1) and the Ton* types (Task 2); register tonPlugin() (Task 12)
  core/model/intent.ts             MODIFY (Task 1): DriverOutput with the address variant (P6-1)
  core/lifecycle/monitor.ts        MODIFY (Task 1): record a driver's failure reason (P6-2)
  core/driver/types.ts             MODIFY (Task 1): includedFinal's `reason`; DriverTxObservation.reason documented
  adapters/ton/
    types.ts       SDK-free types: TonWalletIdentity, TonExt, TonFeeDetails, TonFeeOverride; registry augmentation
    chains.ts      TON_CHAINS (verified data, network params incl. finalitySkewBlocks) and deepFreeze
    presets.ts     TON_PRESETS: `public` (keyless) and `toncenter` (keyed), each rpc (v2) and indexer (v3)
    tokens.ts      TON_TOKENS: the TON alias of the native coin; USDT
    address.ts     strict raw/friendly address codec (SDK-free)
    wallets.ts     wallet identity, canonical key, address, StateInit, signing request, signature placement, TEP-467 hash (SDK)
    messages.ts    comments, native and jetton messages, body decoders, bodies bound to their hash (SDK)
    api.ts         TonApi: toncenter v2/v3 over the transports; tags, routes, validation, quorum keys (SDK-free)
    network.ts     TonNetworkConfig, TON_CAPABILITIES, TON_INDEXER_CAPABILITIES (SDK-free)
    fees.ts        the `ton` fee draft and override (SDK-free)
    errors.ts      broadcast classification (SDK-free)
    trace.ts       the verdict: our own Attempt, requested vs sent, bounces, jetton legs, completeness (SDK)
    decode.ts      V3 transactions into DriverTransaction (SDK)
    reader.ts      TonContext, address codec wiring, ChainReader, SequenceSource, ext.ton, jetton lookups and leg checks (SDK)
    builder.ts     TxBuilder and Broadcaster (SDK)
    proofs.ts      ProofSource and AddressHistorySource (SDK)
    driver.ts      tonDriverFactory(makeNative): probes, ports, limits (SDK through wallets.ts)
    native-client.ts  the library module: tonNativeClient (TonClient over the transport), tonLibraryDriverFactory (SDK)
    plugin.ts      tonPlugin, tonManifest, TON_PEER_DEPENDENCIES keyed by package (SDK-free)
    index.ts       `crypto-aio/ton`: types, TON_PEER_DEPENDENCIES, TON_CAPABILITIES, NativeClientMap augmentation
test/
  core/model/model.test.ts, core/lifecycle/monitor.test.ts        MODIFY (Task 1)
  adapters/ton/
    support/vectors.ts   the test key and frozen vectors (live-cross-checked)
    support/node.ts      ScriptedTonNode (test-only, D19)
    support/harness.ts   tonClock, tonNode, testWallet, signedBoc
    support/context.ts   tonHarness: a driver context over the node
    support/env.ts       createTonEnv (e2e), countingSigner
    data, address, wallets, node, api, policy, trace, reader, builder, proofs, driver, plugin, lazy, dependency, e2e tests
  integration/ton.test.ts   opt-in, read-only
  architecture/registry-augmentation.test.ts   MODIFY (Task 12)
package.json, pnpm-lock.yaml, typedoc.json    MODIFY (Tasks 3, 12)
docs/guides/{index,quick-start,networks,concepts,transactions,security}.md, README.md, CHANGELOG.md   MODIFY (Task 15)
```

No driver request path waits on a real timer (R46): the driver calls `TonApi`, which calls `transport.http` directly (D1). The SDKs appear only in `wallets.ts`, `messages.ts`, `native-client.ts` and the modules that import them, all reached from `native-client.ts`, which only the manifest's `load()` requires (`lazy.test.ts` pins it per manifest).

**Task dependencies at a glance.** 1 (core, lifted into Plan 2.5 by A11) → 2 (data) → 3 (SDK, addresses, wallets) → 4 (node) → 5 (API) → 6 (policy) → 7 (verdict, decoding) → 8 (reader) → 9 (builder) → 10 (proofs, history) → 11 (driver) → 12 (plugin, packaging) → 13 (e2e) → 14 (integration) → 15 (docs). Tasks 1, 12 and 15 touch shared files (see Merge notes).

## Chain-specific risks

| Risk | What goes wrong | How this plan handles it |
|---|---|---|
| **Seqno race** | Two workers (or two Operations) build for the same seqno; only one message can land, the other is invalid forever. | The core's seqno path (`nextSeqno`/`seqnoHolder`) allocates under the address lease and refuses a second live Operation with `SEQUENCE_BUSY` until the first is `included` or proven dead. `build` uses exactly the reserved seqno (`assertBuiltOrdering`), and `sequence.pending` is the larger of the live seqno (0 while undeployed) and 1 + the seqno of the wallet's newest proven request indexed after the live read's block, so a lagging liteserver backend can never hand out a seqno the indexer already saw consumed (I1), and a forged or made-up request can never raise it (A23). Task 8 pins a lagging monitor endpoint; Task 13 pins the race. |
| **Expiry vs "may still land"** | Local time passes `valid_until` while a shard block older than that can still include the message. | `valid_until` comes from chain time (D8); `expired` is proven only when every shard top committed by the quorum-attested masterchain block is at least `valid_until` old (D9), and `includedFinal` then confirms the message was not included. Task 10 pins a lagging shard. |
| **Accepted, never executed** | An undeployed wallet without `StateInit`, an unfunded wallet, a wrong seqno: the liteserver refuses (toncenter: HTTP 500), or a message accepted earlier no longer applies when its block is built, and no transaction ever appears. | Deployment is decided at build from live state; `checkFunds` covers value plus fees; the 500 is ambiguous and rethrown (D16), and the Attempt ends `expired` (proven), never `failed` from absence. Task 13 pins an unfunded wallet and a message dropped before inclusion. |
| **Skipped message (send mode +2)** | The wallet transaction succeeds and the seqno is consumed, but a message the balance cannot pay is skipped: nothing moved. | The verdict compares the signed request's messages with the transaction's outgoing messages (D11); a missing one is `failed` (`the wallet skipped a message`). Review Focus 1. |
| **Bounce semantics** | A bounceable transfer to an uninitialized account (or a failing contract) comes back minus fees; a non-bounceable one stays. | The recipient's address flag decides (D7, P6-1); the verdict follows each message to its destination transaction and fails a bounced one (`transfer bounced`). Review Focus 3. |
| **Jetton outcome lives elsewhere** | The wallet transaction only asks its jetton wallet to transfer; the move can fail two hops later (insufficient jettons, failing recipient wallet) and bounce, or a wrong jetton wallet can "accept" a phantom transfer. | `final` only when the trace is complete, our jetton wallet sent a positive `internal_transfer` from us which the receiving jetton wallet executed, and both jetton wallets are the master's own for us and the intended recipient, read under `PROOF` (D11, I5); otherwise `failed`. Tasks 7, 8 and 13 pin the bounce points and the fake jetton wallet. |
| **Fake jettons** | Any contract can send a `transfer_notification` or claim a master in `get_wallet_data`. | Inbound jetton movements are decoded only for a jetton wallet the master itself returns for the owner (`get_wallet_address`), cached; others are left undecoded (`partial`) (D14). Sending resolves our jetton wallet under `PROOF` (I5). |
| **Trace completion vs indexer lag** | toncenter v3 indexes transactions and traces seconds after the liteserver sees them; a trace looks complete only when every hop is indexed. | `observe` reports `success` only for a complete trace; `includedFinal` decides only for a complete trace whose last block the rpc endpoints attest, and a negative needs attested state plus the consuming message found in a fixed window; everything else throws a retryable error that decides nothing (D10, D12, lesson 16). Review Focus 2. |
| **Non-canonical AttemptRef** | The Attempt id is the normalized external message hash; the transaction hash is known only after indexing, and a false "not found" could turn into a proven `replaced`. | The canonical hash is written into the observation (`txHash`) as soon as the indexer resolves it, and only for the external message to `from` whose locally computed TEP-467 hash is the id (C1); "not included" needs attested state and, for a consumed seqno, the consuming message found and hashed locally from a body bound to its keyed hash (D12); unmanaged lookups accept either hash (`observe` tries the message hash, then the transaction hash). |
| **v5r1 wallet id per network** | The same key has different v5r1 addresses on mainnet and testnet; the SDK's defaults are mainnet's id and workchain 0. | Identity resolution passes every field explicitly, defaults `networkGlobalId` to the network's and refuses another (`CONFIG_INVALID`), and the build refuses a wallet whose derived address is not `from` (D17, lesson 5). Review Focus 4. |
| **Signature placement** | v4r2 puts the signature first, v5r1 last; a wrong placement is a message no wallet accepts. | `assemble` places it only where the remainder hashes to the signed digest (D5), and live mainnet signatures pin the layout (Appendix A). |
| **Lenient SDK address parsing** | `@ton/core` accepts `00:…`, `1:…`, mixed base64 alphabets and odd workchain bytes, and throws a string on a bad tag. | A strict SDK-free codec accepts only the canonical forms, and the SDK only ever sees canonical raw addresses (lesson 4). |
| **Testnet-only addresses on mainnet** | Funds sent to a `kQ…`/`0Q…` address from mainnet software. | Mainnet refuses the testnet flag (TEP-2) with `INVALID_ADDRESS`. |
| **TON-native mnemonics** | Tonkeeper's 24 words are not BIP39; `localSigner.fromMnemonic` derives another key. | The guide says to import the ed25519 seed; `fromPublicKey` makes the mismatch visible as a different address (D17). |
| **Signature domains** | `@ton/core` 0.63 can prefix a signature domain (L2 networks). | The driver never sets one: mainnet and testnet use the empty domain, and the signed digest is the plain cell hash (the node verifies it with `@noble/curves`). |
| **One lying or buggy endpoint** | The transport returns the first endpoint's answer once the quorum key agrees, so any field the key does not cover, or any body its hash does not bind, is that one endpoint's word. | Quorum keys cover the parsed values of every field a proof reads (blocks, accounts, transactions, whole traces); every body read must hash to its keyed `bodyHash`; the Attempt is identified by a locally computed hash (C1, M5). Task 5 and Task 10 pin a liar that flips a bounce, swaps a body and offers another wallet's transaction. |
| **Forged relayed requests** | Anyone can post, for about 0.01 GRAM, an internal message whose body claims to be a W5 `internal_signed` request for any seqno; a v5r1 wallet ignores a forged one in a successful transaction, and a v4r2 wallet accepts any internal body. Taken as evidence, it would prove a landed transfer `replaced` or pin the next seqno above the real one. | A relayed request counts only when its compute phase succeeded and its signature verifies under the wallet's key with a wallet id that derives the wallet (ruling A23); external requests need the same signature and wallet-id check, against a lone lying indexer that makes one up; the seqno floor also needs evidence newer than the live read. Tasks 3, 4, 8 and 10 pin forged, made-up and genuine requests. |
| **Keyless provider limits** | toncenter without a key allows one request per second (a key: 10). | The `public` preset is marked not-for-production and rate-limited to 1 rps, the keyed `toncenter` preset to 10 rps (the guide says how to raise it, M16); health probes take tokens from the bucket (A17, Plan 2.5); the integration test's pauses are a courtesy. |

## Unresolved assumptions

| Assumption | Default chosen | Cost if wrong |
|---|---|---|
| `finalitySkewBlocks` = 10 masterchain blocks (~4 s) covers how far a healthy peer usually trails the freshest endpoint. | D10 attests the unanchored head at head − 10, then once more at head − 150 (the transport's lag tolerance) (M1). | Proofs decide a little later; never a false head. The param is validated 1–1000. |
| The consumer of a seqno lies within the wallet's last 512 transactions at the attested head. | `CONSUMER_PAGES` = 8 × 64 (D12); exhausting it logs `SEQNO_CONSUMER_NOT_FOUND`. | A very busy deposit wallet could keep an Attempt undecided; raise the constant. Never a false verdict. |
| toncenter v3 `/transactions?account=&end_lt=&sort=desc` returns the same page from every honest indexer that has indexed the window. | The consumer search quorum-reads fixed windows, keyed on every parsed field and bound bodies (D12, C1). | Disagreeing pages decide nothing (liveness only). |
| `sync_utime` of `getAddressInformation` is the chain time of the state read. | `valid_until = sync_utime + validForSeconds`, with `sync_utime` within 300 s of the local clock (D8, M3). | Checked live (it tracks the block time within a second); if an endpoint returned a stale time, messages would expire early (`expired`, then `rebuild`), never land late. |
| toncenter's `source_fees.fwd_fee` is the forward fee of the request's outgoing messages; without it, the forward fee is `computeFwdFees` over each message's cells outside the root, from config param 25 (24 when either side is the masterchain), as `@ton/ton` implements it. | D13: the emulated value when positive, else the computed one, never both (I3). | A native fee estimate a little off; the verdict still fails a skipped message honestly. |
| A bounce phase of type `ok` is the only bounce that returns value; `nofunds`/`negfunds` leave the value at the destination. | The verdict fails only `ok` bounces; `nofunds`/`negfunds` count as delivered, and decoding reports an internal message executed unless it bounced back (M7). | A dust-sized bounceable transfer that could not pay for its bounce is reported delivered, which it effectively is (the value stays at the destination). |
| `get_wallet_address` of a standard jetton master is stable for a master and owner. | Cached per container (immutable chain data), and only answers read under the proof quorum are cached (I5). | A master that changes its wallet code would need a container restart. |
| Jetton deposits sent with `forward_ton_amount = 0` send no notification (TEP-74). | `history(owner)` finds jetton deposits through the `transfer_notification` the owner receives; the arrival itself (`internal_transfer`) happens on the jetton wallet's account. | A deposit sent without a notification appears only in the owner's jetton wallet's history; the guide says so (M17). |
| toncenter answers every refused external message with HTTP 500. | Broadcasts are `accepted` or ambiguous (D16). | A 4xx answer is classified by `errors.ts`, default `refused`, never `rejected` except malformed bytes; on a `proof`/`monitor` read it is retryable (M2). |

## Merge notes

Plan 2 is merged into `main` before this plan executes; cut the TON execution branch from that `main`. Shared files:

- **`src/core/model/intent.ts`, `src/core/lifecycle/monitor.ts`, `src/core/driver/types.ts`** (Task 1): additive (P6-1, P6-2 with the M8 clearing of stale reasons, rulings A8/A9), and lifted into Plan 2.5 (A11) together with P3-B, P5-A, the N5 fix (A14) and the AttemptRef guard (A15). Execute this plan on a branch cut after Plan 2.5 merged, and skip Task 1. The A15 guard never fires for TON: an Attempt id covers the wallet, its seqno and `valid_until`, so two live Operations cannot share one.
- **`src/index.ts`**: Task 1 adds `DriverOutput` to the existing `./core/model/intent` type export; Task 2 appends the `Ton*` type export after Plan 2's `Evm*` line; Task 12 appends `tonPlugin()` to Plan 2's `BUILTIN_PLUGINS` array (after `evmPlugin()` and any family merged before) and its import next to Plan 2's. Keep every family's lines.
- **`package.json` / `pnpm-lock.yaml`**: Task 3 adds three exact devDependencies and three optional peers beside Plan 2's; Task 12 adds the `./ton` export and `typesVersions` entry beside `./evm`. Regenerate the lockfile only with pnpm 10.5.2 (`pnpm install`), never by hand.
- **`typedoc.json`**: Task 12 appends `src/adapters/ton/index.ts` to `entryPoints`.
- **`test/architecture/registry-augmentation.test.ts`**: Task 12 adds a `'crypto-aio/ton'` path, a TON typing block after Plan 2's EVM block (both file orders, with `USE_ACME`), and extends the SDK-free main-entry guard (`@ton` hidden, the TON entry in `declarations()`, a TON control).
- **`test/architecture/boundaries.test.ts`**: unchanged; `dependencies` stays the same.
- **`docs/guides/*.md`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`** (Task 15): TON sections go under Plan 2's layout ("Configuring a real network", "More networks of an existing family", the integration matrix, the `[Unreleased]` → `Added` list). Where Plan 2's final headings differ from the ones quoted in Task 15, add the TON text under the matching heading.
- **`src/testing/generation.ts`** (created by Plan 2 Task 11): consumed, never modified (D20).

### Task 1: Core prerequisites: the output address variant (P6-1) and failure reasons (P6-2) (lifted into Plan 2.5)

> **Ruling A11.** This task is self-contained and is lifted into Plan 2.5, which runs after Plan 2 merges and before any family plan. When Plan 2.5 has landed on `main`, skip this task and start at Task 2; every later task only consumes the API below (`DriverOutput.variant`, `includedFinal`'s `reason`, the monitor recording `reason`). If Plan 2.5 changed a name, follow Plan 2.5.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/core/model/intent.ts`, `src/core/lifecycle/monitor.ts`, `src/core/driver/types.ts`, `src/index.ts`
- Test: `test/core/model/model.test.ts`, `test/core/lifecycle/monitor.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `toStoredIntent`, `intentHash`, `NormalizedIntent` (`src/core/model/intent.ts`); `Monitor` (`src/core/lifecycle/monitor.ts`); `createFakeEnv`, `REVERT_ADDRESS` (`src/testing`).
- Produces:
  - `interface DriverOutput { to: string; amount: bigint; variant?: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>> }`, exported from `crypto-aio`; `DriverIntent.outputs: readonly DriverOutput[]`. `toStoredIntent` copies `Address.variant` into each output only when present, so `intentHash` covers it and intents of chains without variants hash exactly as before (controller ruling A8).
  - `ProofSource.includedFinal`'s included branch gains `readonly reason?: string`; `DriverTxObservation.reason` is documented as a fixed text. The monitor records `reason` on an observed failure (`seen.success === false`) and on a proven one (`success: false`), and clears any earlier reason (a refusal, an older failure) on every other observed or proven inclusion, so a success never carries one (ruling A9, M8). Reasons stay sensitive-classified (R24).
- Plan 3's P3-B also adds a member to `src/core/driver/types.ts`; whichever merges second rebases onto the other (ruling A10).

```ts
describe('output variants (P6-1)', () => {
  const on = (chain: string): AssetInfo => ({
    id: `${chain}:mainnet/native`,
    chain,
    network: 'mainnet',
    ref: 'native',
    metadata: { symbol: 'X', decimals: 6 },
  });
  const stored = (chain: string, to: string, from: string, variant?: object) =>
    toStoredIntent({
      asset: on(chain),
      outputs: [
        {
          to: new Address(chain, {
            canonical: to,
            display: to,
            ...(variant ? { variant: variant as Record<string, unknown> } : {}),
          }),
          amount: Amount.fromBase(1234n, on(chain)),
        },
      ],
      from: new Address(chain, { canonical: from, display: from }),
      memo: 'm',
      fee: 'normal',
    });

  it('leaves the intent hash of chains without variants unchanged', () => {
    // Frozen from the code before P6-1: EVM, UTXO, Tron and Solana have no variant.
    const cases: readonly (readonly [string, string, string, string])[] = [
      [
        'ethereum',
        '0xdAC17F958D2ee523a2206206994597C13D831ec7',
        '0x0000000000000000000000000000000000000001',
        '3b4bc23689ba35bccff579071773602e547c796ebb3b1f2cc230ed0ca18b5aea',
      ],
      [
        'bitcoin',
        'bc1qar0srrr7xfkvy5l643lydnw9re59gtzzwf5mdq',
        'bc1qw508d6qejxtdg4y5r3zarvary0c5xw7kv8f3t4',
        '1ad44b6ebad925bfd80e4380db5802ec14a92480f1a0ed1b70f1edb48317864b',
      ],
      [
        'tron',
        'TR7NHqjeKQxGTCi8q8ZY4pL8otSzgjLj6t',
        'TLa2f6VPqDgRE67v1736s7bJ8Ray5wYjU7',
        '57a9a67fddd48de43a513210a0c072e81074c2007c6c32f9ff89e0d8b984297c',
      ],
      [
        'solana',
        'Es9vMFrzaCERmJfrF4H2FYD4KCoNkY11McCe8BenwNYB',
        '11111111111111111111111111111111',
        'c2955f1688bc0cc1c162bb64f2b1d763bb3954046528da5ad72ad12c198be4d0',
      ],
    ];
    for (const [chain, to, from, hash] of cases) {
      const intent = stored(chain, to, from);
      expect(intent.outputs[0]).toEqual({ to, amount: 1234n });
      expect(intentHash(chain, 'mainnet', intent)).toBe(hash);
    }
  });

  it('carries a variant to drivers as a plain copy and hashes it', () => {
    const raw = `0:${'ab'.repeat(32)}`;
    const variant = { bounceable: false, testOnly: false, urlSafe: true };
    const plain = stored('ton', raw, raw);
    const nonBounceable = stored('ton', raw, raw, variant);
    const bounceable = stored('ton', raw, raw, { ...variant, bounceable: true });
    expect(nonBounceable.outputs[0]).toEqual({ to: raw, amount: 1234n, variant });
    expect(Object.isFrozen(nonBounceable.outputs[0]?.variant)).toBe(false);
    const hashes = new Set(
      [plain, nonBounceable, bounceable].map((i) => intentHash('ton', 'mainnet', i)),
    );
    expect(hashes.size).toBe(3);
  });
});
```

Append to `test/core/lifecycle/monitor.test.ts` (it already imports `ReadTarget`, `REVERT_ADDRESS`, `createFakeEnv`, and defines `monitorOf` and `stored`):

```ts

describe('failure reasons (P6-2)', () => {
  /** The handle's driver, with a fixed reason on every failed observation and proof. */
  function withReasons(target: ReadTarget): ReadTarget {
    const { reader, proofs } = target.pooled.driver;
    return {
      ...target,
      pooled: {
        ...target.pooled,
        driver: {
          ...target.pooled.driver,
          reader: {
            ...reader,
            observe: async (ref, ordering, from) => ({
              ...(await reader.observe(ref, ordering, from)),
              reason: 'transfer bounced',
            }),
          },
          proofs: {
            ...proofs,
            includedFinal: async (ref, ordering, from) => {
              const proof = await proofs.includedFinal(ref, ordering, from);
              return proof.included ? { ...proof, reason: 'transfer bounced' } : proof;
            },
          },
        },
      },
    };
  }

  it('records the driver reason of an observed, then a proven, failure', async () => {
    const env = await createFakeEnv();
    const sub = await env.run(env.bc.transfer({ to: REVERT_ADDRESS, amount: 5n }));
    const { monitor, target } = await monitorOf(env);
    const reasoned = withReasons(target);
    env.chain.mine();
    await env.run(monitor.check(reasoned, await stored(env, sub.operationId)));
    const attemptId = (await stored(env, sub.operationId)).activeAttemptId ?? '';
    expect(await env.stores.operations.getObservation(attemptId)).toMatchObject({
      state: 'failed',
      evidence: 'observed',
      reason: 'transfer bounced',
    });
    for (let i = 0; i < 5; i++) env.chain.mine();
    const done = await env.run(
      monitor.check(reasoned, await stored(env, sub.operationId)),
    );
    expect(done.state).toBe('failed');
    expect(await env.stores.operations.getObservation(attemptId)).toMatchObject({
      state: 'failed',
      evidence: 'proven',
      reason: 'transfer bounced',
    });
    expect(await env.run(env.bc.getTransactionStatus(sub.operationId))).toMatchObject({
      state: 'failed',
      reason: 'transfer bounced',
    });
  });

  it('never records a reason for a transaction that succeeded', async () => {
    const env = await createFakeEnv();
    const sub = await env.run(env.bc.transfer({ to: env.stranger(), amount: 5n }));
    const { monitor, target } = await monitorOf(env);
    const reasoned = withReasons(target);
    for (let i = 0; i < 6; i++) env.chain.mine();
    await env.run(monitor.check(reasoned, await stored(env, sub.operationId)));
    const done = await env.run(
      monitor.check(reasoned, await stored(env, sub.operationId)),
    );
    expect(done.state).toBe('final');
    const observation = await env.stores.operations.getObservation(
      done.activeAttemptId ?? '',
    );
    expect(observation?.state).toBe('final');
    expect(observation?.reason).toBeUndefined();
  });

  it('clears a refusal reason once the transaction is proven final (M8)', async () => {
    const env = await createFakeEnv();
    const sub = await env.run(env.bc.transfer({ to: env.stranger(), amount: 5n }));
    env.chain.dropFromMempool(sub.attempt?.id ?? '');
    env.chain.configureEndpoint('main', { refuseNext: 'invalid signature' });
    await expect(env.run(env.bc.rebroadcast(sub.operationId))).rejects.toMatchObject({
      code: 'TX_REFUSED',
    });
    const attemptId = (await stored(env, sub.operationId)).activeAttemptId ?? '';
    expect(await env.stores.operations.getObservation(attemptId)).toMatchObject({
      state: 'refused',
      reason: 'invalid signature',
    });
    expect((await env.run(env.bc.rebroadcast(sub.operationId))).state).toBe('submitted');
    for (let i = 0; i < 6; i++) env.chain.mine();
    const { monitor, target } = await monitorOf(env);
    await env.run(monitor.check(target, await stored(env, sub.operationId)));
    const done = await env.run(monitor.check(target, await stored(env, sub.operationId)));
    expect(done.state).toBe('final');
    expect(await env.stores.operations.getObservation(attemptId)).toMatchObject({
      state: 'final',
      reason: undefined,
    });
    expect(
      (await env.run(env.bc.getTransactionStatus(sub.operationId))).reason,
    ).toBeUndefined();
  });
});
```

```ts
   * P6-2: with `success: false`, why, as a short fixed text (R24: no addresses, amounts or
   * node text). The monitor records it on the observation (`TxStatus.reason`).
   */
  readonly reason?: string;
}
```

and in `ProofSource.includedFinal`'s return type, after `readonly txHash: string;`, add:

```ts
        /** P6-2: with `success: false`, a short fixed text (R24), recorded as proven. */
        readonly reason?: string;
```

In `src/core/lifecycle/monitor.ts`, in `observeAttempt`, replace

```ts
        ...(seen.txHash !== undefined ? { txHash: seen.txHash } : {}),
        firstSeenAt: current?.firstSeenAt ?? now,
        lastSeenAt: now,
      };
      if (seen.blockHeight <= finalized)
```

with

```ts
        ...(seen.txHash !== undefined ? { txHash: seen.txHash } : {}),
        // P6-2: why an included transaction failed, as the driver's fixed text (R24); any
        // earlier reason (a refusal, an old failure) is cleared otherwise (M8).
        reason: seen.success === false ? seen.reason : undefined,
        firstSeenAt: current?.firstSeenAt ?? now,
        lastSeenAt: now,
      };
      if (seen.blockHeight <= finalized)
```

In `proveFinal`, add `reason: failureReason(proof),` after `confirmations: depth(head, proof.blockHeight),`; in `whenAbsent`, add `reason: failureReason(proof),` after `txHash: proof.txHash,` in the returned proven patch. The key is always written, so `undefined` clears a stale reason (the observation patch merges key by key). Append at the end of the file:

```ts

/** P6-2: a proven failure's fixed reason (R24); a success clears any earlier reason (M8). */
function failureReason(proof: {
  readonly success: boolean;
  readonly reason?: string;
}): string | undefined {
  return proof.success ? undefined : proof.reason;
}
```

```ts
describe('TON provider presets', () => {

    chain: 'ton',
    network,
    ...(apiKey !== undefined ? { apiKey: secret(apiKey) } : {}),
  });

    const { presets } = catalogs();
    expect(presets.resolve('public', input('mainnet'), 'rpc')).toMatchObject({
      endpoints: [{ url: 'https://toncenter.com/api/v2', rateLimit: { rps: 1 } }],
      preset: { production: false },
    });
    expect(presets.resolve('public', input('testnet'), 'indexer').endpoints).toEqual([
      {
        name: 'toncenter',
        url: 'https://testnet.toncenter.com/api/v3',
        rateLimit: { rps: 1 },
      },
    ]);
  });

  it('limits the keyed preset to the free-key rate (M16)', () => {
    const { presets } = catalogs();
    expect(
      presets.resolve('toncenter', input('mainnet', 'k'), 'rpc').endpoints[0]?.rateLimit,
    ).toEqual({ rps: 10 });
  });

  it('puts the toncenter key in a secret header, never in the URL', () => {
    const { presets } = catalogs();
    for (const kind of ['rpc', 'indexer'] as const) {
      const [endpoint] = presets.resolve(
        'toncenter',
        input('mainnet', 'k-123'),
        kind,
      ).endpoints;
      expect(String(endpoint?.url)).not.toContain('k-123');
      const header = endpoint?.headers?.['X-API-Key'];
      expect(isSecret(header)).toBe(true);
      expect(reveal(header as never)).toBe('k-123');
      expect(JSON.stringify(endpoint)).not.toContain('k-123');
    }
  });

  it('refuses a missing or empty key, and other chains, with CONFIG_INVALID', () => {
    const { presets } = catalogs();
    expect(() => presets.resolve('toncenter', input('mainnet'), 'rpc')).toThrow(
      expect.objectContaining({ code: 'CONFIG_INVALID' }),
    );
    let error: unknown;
    try {
      presets.resolve('toncenter', input('testnet', '   '), 'indexer');
    } catch (caught) {
      error = caught;
    }
    expect(error).toMatchObject({
      code: 'CONFIG_INVALID',
      message: expect.stringContaining('non-empty apiKey'),
    });
    expect(() =>
      presets.resolve('toncenter', { chain: 'ethereum', network: 'mainnet' }, 'rpc'),
    ).toThrow(expect.objectContaining({ code: 'CONFIG_INVALID' }));
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the SDK-free types**

`src/adapters/ton/types.ts`:

```ts
/**
 * SDK-free types of the TON family: the wallet identity (spec §9), the `ext.ton` API, fee
 * details and overrides, and the transport tags. Nothing here imports an SDK, so the
 * composition root can export these types.
 */
import type { CallOptions } from '../../core/transport/types';

// R37: augment the registries through the package entry, as users do with 'crypto-aio'.
declare module '../../index' {
  interface ChainRegistry {
    ton: { family: 'ton'; network: 'mainnet' | 'testnet' };
  }
  interface FamilyRegistry {
    ton: { library: '@ton/ton'; ext: TonExt; fee: TonFeeDetails };
  }
}

export type TonWalletVersion = 'v4r2' | 'v5r1';

/**
 * The wallet contract behind a TON address (spec §9). Every field determines the address,
 * so it is wallet config (`wallets.<name>.ton`), never a per-call option.
 * - v4r2: `subwalletId` defaults to `698983191 + workchain`.
 * - v5r1: `subwalletNumber` defaults to 0 (15 bits); `networkGlobalId` defaults to the
 *   network's global id (-239 mainnet, -3 testnet) and must equal it.
 */
export type TonWalletIdentity =
  | {
      readonly version: 'v4r2';
      readonly workchain?: 0 | -1;
      readonly subwalletId?: number;
    }
  | {
      readonly version: 'v5r1';
      readonly workchain?: 0 | -1;
      readonly subwalletNumber?: number;
      readonly networkGlobalId?: number;
    };

/** `bc.ext.ton`: the TON family extension (spec §5.5). */
export interface TonExt {
  readonly ton: {
    /** The wallet's seqno at the latest masterchain block; 0 while it is not deployed. */
    getSeqno(address: string): Promise<bigint>;
    /** The jetton wallet that `owner` holds for the jetton `master` (raw form). */
    jettonWallet(owner: string, master: string): Promise<string>;
  };
}

/**
 * `FeeEstimate.details` of the `ton` fee kind, in nanograms. The `network` charge is
 * `importFee + gasFee + storageFee + forwardFee`; a jetton transfer adds an `attached`
 * charge (`attached` per output), whose unspent part the jetton wallet refunds.
 */
export interface TonFeeDetails {
  /** The inbound external message's import fee. */
  readonly importFee: bigint;
  /** The wallet's compute phase, from the endpoint's emulation. */
  readonly gasFee: bigint;
  readonly storageFee: bigint;
  /** The forward fees of every outgoing internal message (full `fwd_fee`), counted once. */
  readonly forwardFee: bigint;
  /**
   * Where `forwardFee` comes from (I3): the endpoint's emulation (`fwd_fee`, which follows
   * the real action list), or, when it reports none, config params 24/25.
   */
  readonly forwardFeeSource: 'emulated' | 'computed';
  /** Jetton transfers: the value attached to each jetton wallet message. */
  readonly attached?: bigint;
  /** Jetton transfers: `forward_ton_amount`, which pays for the recipient's notification. */
  readonly forwardAmount?: bigint;
  /** Whether the message deploys the wallet (its first send carries the `StateInit`). */
  readonly deploy: boolean;
}

/**
 * An explicit TON fee (`TransferIntent.fee`). TON fees are fixed by the network config, so
 * there is nothing to bid; a jetton transfer may change the value attached to each jetton
 * wallet message (default: the network's `jettonAttached`).
 */
export interface TonFeeOverride {
  readonly attached?: bigint;
}

/**
 * The transport tags every TON I/O call carries (R41). A caller's `quorumKey` replaces the
 * call's default consensus key (lesson 17): proofs attest a monotone predicate, such as
 * "my seqno is above n", instead of comparing data read at a moving head.
 */
export type TonCallTags = Pick<
  CallOptions,
  'purpose' | 'retry' | 'quorum' | 'quorumKey' | 'fanout' | 'signal'
>;
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the chain data, presets and tokens**

`src/adapters/ton/chains.ts`:

```ts
/**
 * The built-in TON chain (spec §2): mainnet and testnet. Every value is verified against the
 * source named in the Plan 6 appendix, or is a documented library policy:
 * - `identity`: the network's global id (config param 19), which the identity probe reads.
 * - The native coin is Gram (ticker GRAM, formerly Toncoin), 9 decimals; the plugin also
 *   registers the alias `TON` for it.
 * - `finality: masterchain`: a masterchain block is final once it exists (BFT).
 * - `maxLagBlocks: 150`: about 60 s of masterchain blocks (measured ~0.4 s apart).
 * - `reorgWindow: 16`: unused; TON has no block source (sharded, spec §15).
 * - `params`: `validForSeconds` (a message's lifetime, from chain time), `jettonAttached`
 *   and `jettonForwardAmount` (nanograms per jetton transfer), `finalitySkewBlocks` (how far
 *   a healthy proof peer may trail the freshest endpoint), all library policy.
 */
import type { ChainInfo, NetworkInfo } from '../../core/model/chain';

function tonNetwork(
  id: string,
  globalId: number,
  testnet: boolean,
  explorer: string,
): NetworkInfo {
  return {
    id,
    identity: String(globalId),
    testnet,
    feeModel: 'ton',
    finality: { kind: 'masterchain' },
    defaultConfirmations: 1,
    reorgWindow: 16,
    maxLagBlocks: 150,
    explorer: { tx: `${explorer}/transaction/{id}`, address: `${explorer}/{address}` },
    params: {
      validForSeconds: 60,
      jettonAttached: 50_000_000n,
      jettonForwardAmount: 1n,
      finalitySkewBlocks: 10,
    },
  };
}

/** R56: exported data is frozen all the way down. */
export function deepFreeze<T>(value: T): T {
  if (value !== null && typeof value === 'object') {
    for (const item of Object.values(value)) deepFreeze(item);
    Object.freeze(value);
  }
  return value;
}

export const TON_CHAINS: readonly ChainInfo[] = deepFreeze([
  {
    id: 'ton',
    family: 'ton',
    model: 'account',
    ordering: 'seqno',
    schemes: ['ed25519'],
    nativeAsset: { symbol: 'GRAM', decimals: 9, name: 'Gram' },
    defaultNetwork: 'mainnet',
    networks: {
      mainnet: tonNetwork('mainnet', -239, false, 'https://tonviewer.com'),
      testnet: tonNetwork('testnet', -3, true, 'https://testnet.tonviewer.com'),
    },
  },
]);
```

`src/adapters/ton/presets.ts`:

```ts
/**
 * TON provider presets (spec §11): toncenter's API v2 (a liteserver proxy, the `rpc`
 * endpoint) and API v3 (its indexer). Base URLs, the `X-API-Key` header and the limits (1
 * request per second keyless, 10 with a free key) are verified (Plan 6 appendix). The key travels in a header
 * whose value is a `Secret`, so it never reaches logs, errors or events.
 */
import { ConfigError } from '../../core/errors/error';
import type { PresetInput, ProviderPreset } from '../../core/registry/providers';
import { reveal, secret } from '../../core/secret/secret';
import type { EndpointConfig } from '../../core/transport/types';
import { deepFreeze } from './chains';

const HOSTS: Readonly<Record<string, string>> = Object.freeze({
  mainnet: 'https://toncenter.com',
  testnet: 'https://testnet.toncenter.com',
});

const API: Readonly<Record<'rpc' | 'indexer', string>> = Object.freeze({
  rpc: '/api/v2',
  indexer: '/api/v3',
});

const supports = (chain: string, network: string): boolean =>
  chain === 'ton' && HOSTS[network] !== undefined;

function baseUrl(kind: 'rpc' | 'indexer', input: PresetInput): string {
  const host = HOSTS[input.network];
  // Unreachable through the catalog, which asks `supports` first.
  if (host === undefined) throw new Error(`no toncenter host for ${input.network}`);
  return `${host}${API[kind]}`;
}

/** The revealed key; the error names the preset and network, never the key. */
function apiKeyOf(input: PresetInput): string {
  const key: unknown = input.apiKey === undefined ? undefined : reveal(input.apiKey);
  if (typeof key !== 'string' || key.trim() === '') {
    throw new ConfigError(
      'CONFIG_INVALID',
      `provider preset 'toncenter' requires a non-empty apiKey for ${input.chain}:${input.network}`,
    );
  }
  return key;
}

function keyless(kind: 'rpc' | 'indexer'): ProviderPreset {
  return {
    name: 'public',
    kind,
    production: false,
    supports,
    endpoints: (input): readonly EndpointConfig[] => [
      { name: 'toncenter', url: baseUrl(kind, input), rateLimit: { rps: 1 } },
    ],
  };
}

function keyed(kind: 'rpc' | 'indexer'): ProviderPreset {
  return {
    name: 'toncenter',
    kind,
    requiresApiKey: true,
    supports,
    endpoints: (input): readonly EndpointConfig[] => [
      {
        name: 'toncenter',
        url: baseUrl(kind, input),
        headers: { 'X-API-Key': secret(apiKeyOf(input)) },
        // The free-key limit (docs.ton.org/api/rate-limit); a paid key overrides it with a
        // custom endpoint config.
        rateLimit: { rps: 10 },
      },
    ],
  };
}

export const TON_PRESETS: readonly ProviderPreset[] = deepFreeze([
  keyless('rpc'),
  keyless('indexer'),
  keyed('rpc'),
  keyed('indexer'),
]);
```

`src/adapters/ton/tokens.ts`:

```ts
/**
 * Well-known TON assets (spec §6.2). USDT is Tether's jetton on TON mainnet (master and
 * 6 decimals verified against docs.ton.org and the master's on-chain content). The native
 * coin keeps the alias `TON` beside its ticker `GRAM` (formerly Toncoin).
 * Jetton asset ids use the master's raw address: `ton:mainnet/jetton:0:<hex>`.
 */
import type { AssetRegistration } from '../../core/registry/assets';
import { TON_CHAINS, deepFreeze } from './chains';

const NATIVE_ALIASES: readonly AssetRegistration[] = Object.keys(
  TON_CHAINS[0]?.networks ?? {},
).map((network) => ({
  chain: 'ton',
  network,
  ref: 'native',
  metadata: { symbol: 'GRAM', decimals: 9, name: 'Gram' },
  aliases: ['TON'],
}));

export const TON_TOKENS: readonly AssetRegistration[] = deepFreeze([
  ...NATIVE_ALIASES,
  {
    chain: 'ton',
    network: 'mainnet',
    ref: {
      standard: 'jetton',
      contract: '0:b113a994b5024a16719f69139328eb759596c38a25f59028b146fecdc3621dfe',
    },
    metadata: { symbol: 'USDT', decimals: 6, name: 'Tether USD' },
    aliases: ['USDT'],
  },
]);
```

- Test: `test/adapters/ton/address.test.ts`, `test/adapters/ton/wallets.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `AddressCodec`, `WalletOptions` (`src/core/driver/types.ts`), `NormalizedAddress`, `ValidationError`, `ConfigError`, `SigningError`, `ProviderError`; `@noble/curves` `ed25519`; `@ton/core` (`Address`, `Cell`, `beginCell`, `external`, `internal`, `comment`, `loadMessage`, `loadMessageRelaxed`, `loadOutList`, `storeMessage`, `SendMode`); `@ton/ton` (`WalletContractV4`, `WalletContractV5R1`).
- Produces:
  - `address.ts` (SDK-free): `type TonWorkchain = 0 | -1`; `type TonAddressVariant = { bounceable; testOnly; urlSafe }`; `crc16(data): number`; `rawAddress(workchain, hash): string`; `friendlyAddress(workchain, hash, flags): string`; `parseTonAddress(value): ParsedTonAddress | null`; `createTonAddressCodec({ testnet, fromPublicKey }): AddressCodec` (canonical raw lower case; `display` keeps the caller's text; `variant` the flags; raw = bounceable with the network's test flag; mainnet refuses testnet-only; `format(address, { bounceable?, testOnly?, urlSafe?, raw? })`).
  - `wallets.ts` (SDK): `MAX_MESSAGES = { v4r2: 4, v5r1: 255 }`; `SEND_MODE` (3); `type TonIdentity`; `resolveIdentity(wallet: WalletOptions | undefined, globalId): TonIdentity`; `walletAddress(identity, publicKey): string` (the key must be a canonical ed25519 point of full order, else `CONFIG_INVALID`, M12); `walletStateInit(identity, publicKey): StateInit`; `walletIdOf(identity, publicKey): number`; `unsignedRequest(identity, publicKey, { seqno, validUntil, messages, deploy }): Promise<{ message: Cell; digest: Uint8Array }>`; `signedRequest(unsigned: Cell, digest, signature): Cell`; `normalizedHash(externalMessage: Cell): Uint8Array`; `externalHashOf(account, body): string` (the TEP-467 hash, hex, of the external message `body` to `account` travels in: C1 computes the Attempt id locally); `requestIsOwn(from, body, publicKey, globalId): boolean` (A23 and the final review: a wallet request, external or relayed, whose ed25519 signature verifies under the wallet's key and whose wallet id derives `from` with that key; v4r2 signs first, v5r1 last); `sdkAddress(raw): Address`. Its errors are `ValidationError`/`SigningError` with fixed texts, never a bare `Error` (M12).
  - `messages.ts` (SDK): `OP`; `MAX_MEMO_BYTES` (1024); `memoBytes(memo)`; `commentCell(memo)`; `nativeMessage({ to, value, bounce, memo? })`; `jettonMessage({ jettonWallet, attached, queryId, amount, destination, responseDestination, forwardAmount, memo? })`; `addressArgument(raw)`; `addressFromBoc(boc)`; `decodeComment(body)`; `decodeJettonTransfer(body)`; `decodeJettonInternalTransfer(body)`; `decodeJettonNotification(body)`; `decodeWalletRequest(body): WalletRequest | null` (`{ auth: 'external' | 'internal'; walletId; seqno; validUntil; messages }`: a v4r2 or v5r1 external request, or a W5 `internal_signed` one relayed internally, M17, which, like any request, proves nothing until `requestIsOwn` authenticates it); `messageFacts(message)`; `cellFromBoc(boc)`; `messageBody({ body?, bodyHash? }): Cell | null` (C1: a body that does not hash to its keyed `bodyHash` is a retryable `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`).
  - Test support `vectors.ts`: `KEY`/`PUBLIC_KEY` (RFC 8032 test 1), `TEST_WALLETS`, `WALLET_IDS`, `CHAIN_WALLETS`, `REAL_REQUESTS`, `USDT_MASTER`, `MASTERCHAIN_FRIENDLY`.

```ts
/**
 * Frozen TON vectors. Cross-checks (Plan 6 appendix):
 * - `KEY`/`PUBLIC_KEY`: RFC 8032 §7.1 test 1, so the ed25519 key pair is independent.
 * - `CHAIN_WALLETS`: live mainnet wallets (toncenter `walletInformation` type and
 *   `get_public_key`); our derivation must reproduce the address the chain holds.
 * - `REAL_REQUESTS`: live mainnet external messages whose signatures verify under
 *   `@noble/curves` over our digest layout, with the node's raw and TEP-467 hashes.
 * - `WALLET_IDS`: the v5r1 wallet ids seen on each live network.
 */
```
